A long 'ticket block' reason is externalized to .saipen/recovery/log-detail/ (lossless, COMMITTED, correct sha) and the LIVE log line keeps a truncated 'structural_event:' value. fast_check.detail_authority_valid then sees a compact detail reference whose second element is None and answers False, so the structural gate refuses the whole checkpoint with 'the canonical active-block event E-1835 has a missing, malformed, unbound, or non-atomic detail proof' -- even though the detail proof is present and intact on disk. The two rules that both own the live log disagree: one says any long event may be externalized, the other says the structural event must be readable in place. Re-blocking T-132 with a short reason restored CURRENT_PASS immediately, which is the workaround, not the fix

What the sweep lost is the weak claim 'somebody mentioned this file somewhere in prose'; what it kept and strengthened is 'a receipt captured these exact bytes'. The ten files that moved to LEGACY_UNREGISTERED_BEACON were never captured, so the exemption is the truthful label, and the check can no longer be satisfied by writing a filename in a sentence. Known ceiling, deliberately left: the binding table is edited inside a test file, so capturing a beacon now means two steps (start --file, then add the row) instead of one, and until the row is added the sweep reports a correctly captured file. That is loud rather than silent, which is the right way round for this invariant; it would be fixed properly by a source-path field in the intake grammar, which belongs to SAIPEN and not to this repository

(3) Workspace carries both private keys, so self-seal (sender=recipient=same seat) needs no registered peer and keeps sender identity, SENV2 sealing, outbox, Post Office delivery, receipts, dedup, UNREAD/READ state and restart durability canonical. (4) no prune/purge/ephemeral-classification cleanup logic exists in saimail/, so no durable-ownership fix is needed. (5) seed bundle operator_import/future_letters/SAIMAIL_FUTURE_LETTER_GPT56SOL_20261003_1535.zip verified independently: 4 members, AES-256-GCM with bundled 32-byte recovery key, AAD SAIMAIL-FUTURE-LETTER-BOOTSTRAP-v1, recovered plaintext 1493 bytes sha256 f5f2597ae17907c3cd5e57d34d41ce1423bd19716f2d07517c10eeb75346b0a5 == MANIFEST plaintext_sha256 == handoff expected hash.
